import Physlib.Particles.StandardModel.HiggsBoson.Potential
import NyquistHiggsDuality

/-!
# Phase B Bridge 1 — Direct Upstream `Physlib` Quartic Potential Integration (`PhyslibHiggsBridge.lean`)

This module imports `Physlib.Particles.StandardModel.HiggsBoson.Potential` directly from
`leanprover-community/physlib` and bridges it with `NyquistHiggsDuality.lean`.
It certifies the **algebraic isomorphism** between the localized Ginzburg–Landau shock-core
defect condensate potential `V_eff(|c|²) = σ₀ |c|² + λ |c|⁴` (where `σ₀ < 0` inside over-steepened
shock cores and `σ₀ > 0` in the smooth sub-Nyquist bulk) and `Physlib`'s quartic potential
`StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential.toFun`:

1. Under the parameter map `toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam = ⟨-σ₀, lam⟩` (`μ² = -σ₀`, `𝓵 = λ`),
   upstream `StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential.toFun φ x` is identically equal to the
   Bekenstein–Nyquist defect-condensate free-energy density
   `nyquistEffectivePotential σ₀ lam (‖φ‖_H^2 x)` at every spacetime point `x : SpaceTime`.
2. Applying upstream `StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential.isMinOn_iff_field_of_μSq_nonpos_𝓵_pos`
   and `StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential.isMinOn_iff_field_of_μSq_nonneg_𝓵_pos` directly:
   - In the **sub-Nyquist bulk regime** (`0 ≤ σ₀`), the potential minimum occurs if and
     only if the clock section vanishes (`φ x = 0`).
   - In the **overloaded super-Nyquist shock-core regime** (`σ₀ ≤ 0`), the potential minimum occurs
     if and only if the pointwise squared norm equals the equilibrium defect-condensate norm
     `‖φ‖_H^2 x = equilibriumClockNormSq σ₀ lam = -σ₀ / (2 * lam)`, restoring the sonic horizon
     `dV_dcNormSq σ₀ lam (‖φ‖_H^2 x) = 0`.
-/

namespace BekensteinNyquist

open StandardModel
open StandardModel.HiggsField

/-- Map the Bekenstein–Nyquist bare saturation `σ₀` and quartic stiffness `lam` directly into
    an upstream `Physlib` `StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential` structure (`μ2 = -σ₀`, `𝓵 = lam`). -/
def toPhyslibPotential (σ₀ lam : ℝ) : StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential where
  μ2 := -σ₀
  𝓵 := lam

/-- **Exact Pointwise Equivalence with Upstream `Physlib` `Potential.toFun`**:
    For any smooth section `φ : HiggsField` and spacetime point `x : SpaceTime`,
    `(toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).toFun φ x` equals `nyquistEffectivePotential σ₀ lam (‖φ‖_H^2 x)`. -/
theorem toPhyslibPotential_toFun_eq_nyquistEffectivePotential
    (σ₀ lam : ℝ) (φ : HiggsField) (x : SpaceTime) :
    (toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).toFun φ x =
      nyquistEffectivePotential σ₀ lam (‖φ‖_H^2 x) := by
  simp only [StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential.toFun, toPhyslibPotential,
    nyquistEffectivePotential]
  ring

/-- **Upstream `Physlib` Sub-Nyquist Vacuum Theorem (`0 ≤ σ₀`)**:
    Directly invokes `StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential.isMinOn_iff_field_of_μSq_nonpos_𝓵_pos`
    to prove that in the smooth sub-Nyquist regime (`0 ≤ σ₀`), `(φ, x)` minimizes the potential
    on `SpaceTime` if and only if `φ x = 0`. -/
theorem subNyquist_physlib_isMinOn_iff_zero
    (σ₀ lam : ℝ) (hσ : 0 ≤ σ₀) (hlam : 0 < lam) (φ : HiggsField) (x : SpaceTime) :
    IsMinOn (fun (φ, x) => (toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).toFun φ x) Set.univ (φ, x) ↔
      φ x = 0 := by
  have hμ2 : (toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).μ2 ≤ 0 := by
    simp [toPhyslibPotential, hσ]
  exact (toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).isMinOn_iff_field_of_μSq_nonpos_𝓵_pos hlam hμ2 φ x

/-- **Upstream `Physlib` Super-Nyquist Clock-Condensate Theorem (`σ₀ ≤ 0`)**:
    Directly invokes `StandardModel.HiggsField.Potential.isMinOn_iff_field_of_μSq_nonneg_𝓵_pos`
    to prove that when the Nyquist–Margolus–Levitin ceiling is breached (`σ₀ ≤ 0`), `(φ, x)`
    minimizes the potential on `SpaceTime` if and only if
    `‖φ‖_H^2 x = equilibriumClockNormSq σ₀ lam`. -/
theorem superNyquist_physlib_isMinOn_iff_condensate
    (σ₀ lam : ℝ) (hσ : σ₀ ≤ 0) (hlam : 0 < lam) (φ : HiggsField) (x : SpaceTime) :
    IsMinOn (fun (φ, x) => (toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).toFun φ x) Set.univ (φ, x) ↔
      ‖φ‖_H^2 x = equilibriumClockNormSq σ₀ lam := by
  have hμ2 : 0 ≤ (toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).μ2 := by
    simp [toPhyslibPotential, hσ]
  exact (toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).isMinOn_iff_field_of_μSq_nonneg_𝓵_pos hlam hμ2 φ x

/-- **Upstream `Physlib` Sonic-Horizon Restoration at the Potential Minimum**:
    Whenever `(φ, x)` minimizes `(toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).toFun` in the super-Nyquist regime
    (`σ₀ ≤ 0`, `0 < lam`), the effective Bekenstein–Nyquist saturation functional vanishes
    identically (`dV_dcNormSq σ₀ lam (‖φ‖_H^2 x) = 0`), restoring the sonic horizon. -/
theorem superNyquist_physlib_horizon_restoration
    (σ₀ lam : ℝ) (hσ : σ₀ ≤ 0) (hlam : 0 < lam) (φ : HiggsField) (x : SpaceTime)
    (hmin : IsMinOn (fun (φ, x) => (toPhyslibPotential σ₀ lam).toFun φ x) Set.univ (φ, x)) :
    dV_dcNormSq σ₀ lam (‖φ‖_H^2 x) = 0 := by
  have hnorm : ‖φ‖_H^2 x = equilibriumClockNormSq σ₀ lam :=
    (superNyquist_physlib_isMinOn_iff_condensate σ₀ lam hσ hlam φ x).mp hmin
  rw [hnorm]
  exact superNyquist_horizon_restoration σ₀ lam (ne_of_gt hlam)

end BekensteinNyquist

import QuantumInfo.Channels.CPTP
import QuantumInfo.Entropy.VonNeumann
import UnitaryBandwidthDepth
import NyquistRotorGates

/-!
# Phase B Bridge 2 — Direct Upstream `QuantumInfo` CPTP & Entropy Integration (`QuantumInfoClockBridge.lean`)

This module imports `QuantumInfo.Channels.CPTP` and `QuantumInfo.Entropy.VonNeumann` directly from
`leanprover-community/physlib` and bridges them with our Bekenstein–Nyquist formalization
(`UnitaryBandwidthDepth.lean` and `NyquistRotorGates.lean`):

1. **Bipartite Unitary Rotor Evolution as an Upstream `CPTPMap` (`bipartiteUnitaryChannel`)**:
   Maps any fixed-angle (linear) unitary operator `U : 𝐔[d_spatial × d_clock]` (and specifically our
   fixed-angle `givensRotor` and `milestone4TripartiteRouter` gates on their single-excitation spaces)
   into an upstream `CPTPMap` via `CPTPMap.ofUnitary`, certifying `IsCompletelyPositive` and
   `IsTracePreserving`.
   - **Direct Sum (`⊕`) vs. Tensor Product (`⊗`) Embedding:** In `UnitaryBandwidthDepth.lean` and
     Section 1 below (`givensRotorCPTP` on `BlockIdx 1 1 = Fin 1 ⊕ Fin 1` and
     `milestone4TripartiteCPTP` on `TripartiteIdx`), the classical hydrodynamic wave and single-excitation
     quantum sector are formulated on a **direct sum** `d_spatial ⊕ d_clock`, where norm transfers
     between orthogonal subspaces via block off-diagonal leakage `A = (1 - P) U P`. In Sections 2 and 3
     below (`reducedSpatialFluidChannel` and `spatial_shock_entropy_eq_clock_entropy`), we pass to the
     **bipartite quantum tensor product** `d_spatial × d_clock` (related to the direct sum by the
     single-excitation sector embedding `|i⟩_S ↦ |i⟩_S ⊗ |0⟩_C, |c⟩_C ↦ |0⟩_S ⊗ |c⟩_C`) where tracing out
     the clock subsystem via `CPTPMap.traceRight` yields a genuine mixed spatial state with von Neumann
     entanglement entropy `Sᵥₙ`. Note also that `CPTPMap.ofUnitary` applies to fixed-angle (linear)
     unitary gates, whereas the macroscopic Burgers lattice uses a state-dependent mean-field angle rule.
2. **Reduced Spatial Fluid Channel via Partial Trace (`reducedSpatialFluidChannel`)**:
   Constructs the open-system spatial fluid evolution obtained by preparing an ancilla clock state
   `ρ_clock : MState d_clock`, applying the joint unitary `U : 𝐔[d_spatial × d_clock]`, and tracing
   out the clock fiber via `CPTPMap.traceRight`.
3. **Stinespring / Unitary Purification Duality (`spatialChannel_admits_unitary_purification`)**:
   Directly invokes upstream `CPTPMap.purify_IsUnitary` and `CPTPMap.purify_trace` to prove that
   every completely positive trace-preserving channel on the bandlimited spatial fluid arises as the
   partial trace of a strictly unitary evolution on a dilated Hilbert space.
4. **Zero Global Landauer Entropy vs. Apparent Lax Shock Entropy (Monograph Section 3.3)**:
   Directly invokes upstream `Sᵥₙ_of_pure_zero`, `Sᵥₙ_of_partial_eq`, and `Sᵥₙ_nonneg` to prove that
   for any pure joint fluid+clock state `Ψ : Ket (d_spatial × d_clock)`:
   - Global von Neumann entropy vanishes identically (`Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ) = 0` — zero Landauer erasure).
   - Apparent spatial shock entropy `Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ).traceRight` equals the worldline clock
     fiber entropy `Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ).traceLeft` (`≥ 0`), and the quantum mutual information
     `qMutualInfo (MState.pure Ψ)` equals `2 * Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ).traceRight`.
-/

namespace BekensteinNyquist

noncomputable section

variable {d_spatial d_clock : Type*}
variable [Fintype d_spatial] [DecidableEq d_spatial]
variable [Fintype d_clock] [DecidableEq d_clock]

/-! ### 1. Bipartite Unitary Evolution & Concrete Givens / Tripartite Channels in `CPTPMap` -/

/-- The upstream `CPTPMap` induced by a bipartite unitary `U : 𝐔[d_spatial × d_clock]`. -/
def bipartiteUnitaryChannel (U : 𝐔[d_spatial × d_clock]) :
    CPTPMap (d_spatial × d_clock) (d_spatial × d_clock) :=
  CPTPMap.ofUnitary U

/-- `bipartiteUnitaryChannel U` satisfies the upstream `CPTPMap.IsUnitary` predicate. -/
theorem bipartiteUnitaryChannel_isUnitary (U : 𝐔[d_spatial × d_clock]) :
    (bipartiteUnitaryChannel U).IsUnitary :=
  ⟨U, rfl⟩

/-- **Concrete `SU(2)` Givens Rotor as an Upstream `CPTPMap`**:
    Every impedance-matched Givens rotation `givensRotor ϑ ζ` (`‖ζ‖ = 1`) from `NyquistRotorGates.lean`
    defines a unitary `CPTPMap (BlockIdx 1 1) (BlockIdx 1 1)` via `CPTPMap.ofUnitary`. -/
def givensRotorCPTP (ϑ : ℝ) (ζ : ℂ) (hζ : star ζ * ζ = 1) :
    CPTPMap (BlockIdx 1 1) (BlockIdx 1 1) :=
  CPTPMap.ofUnitary ⟨givensRotor ϑ ζ, givensRotor_mem_unitaryGroup ϑ ζ hζ⟩

/-- `givensRotorCPTP ϑ ζ hζ` is a unitary CPTP channel. -/
theorem givensRotorCPTP_isUnitary (ϑ : ℝ) (ζ : ℂ) (hζ : star ζ * ζ = 1) :
    (givensRotorCPTP ϑ ζ hζ).IsUnitary :=
  ⟨⟨givensRotor ϑ ζ, givensRotor_mem_unitaryGroup ϑ ζ hζ⟩, rfl⟩

/-- **Milestone 4 Tripartite 3-Mode Haar Router as an Upstream `CPTPMap`**:
    The composite 2-stage collision router `milestone4TripartiteRouter ϑ₁ θ_rec` on
    `TripartiteIdx = (ū_b, u_{b,UV}, c_slot)` defines a unitary `CPTPMap TripartiteIdx TripartiteIdx`. -/
def milestone4TripartiteCPTP (ϑ₁ θ_rec : ℝ) :
    CPTPMap TripartiteIdx TripartiteIdx :=
  CPTPMap.ofUnitary ⟨milestone4TripartiteRouter ϑ₁ θ_rec,
    milestone4TripartiteRouter_mem_unitaryGroup ϑ₁ θ_rec⟩

/-- `milestone4TripartiteCPTP ϑ₁ θ_rec` is a unitary CPTP channel. -/
theorem milestone4TripartiteCPTP_isUnitary (ϑ₁ θ_rec : ℝ) :
    (milestone4TripartiteCPTP ϑ₁ θ_rec).IsUnitary :=
  ⟨⟨milestone4TripartiteRouter ϑ₁ θ_rec,
    milestone4TripartiteRouter_mem_unitaryGroup ϑ₁ θ_rec⟩, rfl⟩

/-! ### 2. Reduced Spatial Fluid Channel via Partial Trace (`traceRight`) -/

/-- Given a bipartite unitary `U : 𝐔[d_spatial × d_clock]` and an initial clock state
    `ρ_clock : MState d_clock`, the reduced spatial fluid channel is formed by:
    1. appending `Unit` (`ofEquiv (Equiv.prodPUnit d_spatial).symm`),
    2. preparing `ρ_clock` on the right factor (`id ⊗ᶜᵖ replacement ρ_clock`),
    3. evolving jointly under `ofUnitary U`, and
    4. tracing out the clock fiber (`CPTPMap.traceRight`). -/
def reducedSpatialFluidChannel (U : 𝐔[d_spatial × d_clock]) (ρ_clock : MState d_clock) :
    CPTPMap d_spatial d_spatial :=
  let append : CPTPMap d_spatial (d_spatial × Unit) :=
    CPTPMap.ofEquiv (Equiv.prodPUnit d_spatial).symm
  let prep : CPTPMap (d_spatial × Unit) (d_spatial × d_clock) :=
    CPTPMap.id ⊗ᶜᵖ CPTPMap.replacement ρ_clock
  CPTPMap.traceRight ∘ₘ CPTPMap.ofUnitary U ∘ₘ prep ∘ₘ append

/-- **Exact Trace Preservation of the Reduced Spatial Fluid Channel**:
    Tracing out the worldline clock fiber preserves the total trace of any spatial operator
    `X : Matrix d_spatial d_spatial ℂ` (`(reducedSpatialFluidChannel U ρ_clock).map X` has trace `X.trace`). -/
theorem reducedSpatialFluidChannel_tracePreserving
    (U : 𝐔[d_spatial × d_clock]) (ρ_clock : MState d_clock)
    (X : Matrix d_spatial d_spatial ℂ) :
    ((reducedSpatialFluidChannel U ρ_clock).map X).trace = X.trace :=
  (reducedSpatialFluidChannel U ρ_clock).TP X

/-- **Unitary Purification / Stinespring Dilation of Any Spatial CPTP Channel**:
    By upstream `CPTPMap.purify_IsUnitary` and `CPTPMap.purify_trace`, every CPTP channel
    `Λ : CPTPMap d_spatial d_spatial` on the bandlimited spatial subspace admits a unitary channel
    `Λ.purify` on `d_spatial × d_spatial × d_spatial` whose partial trace recovers `Λ`. -/
theorem spatialChannel_admits_unitary_purification [Inhabited d_spatial]
    (Λ : CPTPMap d_spatial d_spatial) :
    Λ.purify.IsUnitary ∧
    Λ = (
      let zero_prep : CPTPMap Unit (d_spatial × d_spatial) :=
        CPTPMap.replacement (MState.pure (Ket.basis default))
      let prep := CPTPMap.id ⊗ᶜᵖ zero_prep
      let append : CPTPMap d_spatial (d_spatial × Unit) :=
        CPTPMap.ofEquiv (Equiv.prodPUnit d_spatial).symm
      CPTPMap.traceLeft ∘ₘ CPTPMap.traceLeft ∘ₘ Λ.purify ∘ₘ prep ∘ₘ append
    ) :=
  ⟨CPTPMap.purify_IsUnitary Λ, CPTPMap.purify_trace Λ⟩

/-! ### 3. Zero Global Landauer Entropy vs. Apparent Lax Shock Entropy (Section 3.3) -/

/-- **Zero Global Landauer Entropy Production**:
    For any pure bipartite fluid+clock state `Ψ : Ket (d_spatial × d_clock)`, the global
    von Neumann entropy vanishes identically (`Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ) = 0`). -/
theorem bipartite_pure_global_entropy_zero (Ψ : Ket (d_spatial × d_clock)) :
    Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ) = 0 :=
  Sᵥₙ_of_pure_zero Ψ

/-- **Apparent Lax Shock Entropy Equals Worldline Clock Entanglement Entropy**:
    When a pure bipartite state `Ψ : Ket (d_spatial × d_clock)` is projected onto the spatial fluid
    by tracing out the clock register (`(MState.pure Ψ).traceRight`), its apparent macroscopic
    von Neumann entropy equals the von Neumann entropy stored in the clock register
    (`(MState.pure Ψ).traceLeft`), and both are non-negative. -/
theorem spatial_shock_entropy_eq_clock_entropy (Ψ : Ket (d_spatial × d_clock)) :
    Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ).traceRight = Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ).traceLeft ∧
    0 ≤ Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ).traceRight :=
  ⟨(Sᵥₙ_of_partial_eq Ψ).symm, Sᵥₙ_nonneg (MState.pure Ψ).traceRight⟩

/-- **Spatial–Clock Quantum Mutual Information Identity**:
    For any pure bipartite fluid+clock state `Ψ : Ket (d_spatial × d_clock)`, the quantum mutual
    information `qMutualInfo (MState.pure Ψ)` between the spatial fluid and the worldline clock
    register is exactly twice the apparent spatial shock entropy:
    `qMutualInfo (MState.pure Ψ) = 2 * Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ).traceRight`. -/
theorem bipartite_pure_mutual_info_eq_two_mul_spatial_entropy
    (Ψ : Ket (d_spatial × d_clock)) :
    qMutualInfo (MState.pure Ψ) = 2 * Sᵥₙ (MState.pure Ψ).traceRight := by
  unfold qMutualInfo
  rw [Sᵥₙ_of_pure_zero Ψ, Sᵥₙ_of_partial_eq Ψ]
  ring

end

end BekensteinNyquist

\title{\textbf{Theoretical Architecture, Axiomatic Foundations, and Formal Verification of the Bekenstein--Nyquist Shock Worldline Conjecture}}
\author{Bekenstein--Nyquist Research Program}
\date{October 2026}
\begin{document}
\sloppy
\maketitle
\begin{abstract}
The Bekenstein--Nyquist Shock Worldline Conjecture (BNSWC) establishes a rigorous mathematical bridge between bandlimited effective field theory (EFT), microlocal partial differential equation (PDE) analysis, and Boolean or unitary circuit complexity. Motivated by three foundational principles---the Bekenstein--Kempf spatial bandwidth cutoff ($\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$)~\cite{bekenstein1981,casini2008,kempf2004}, the Margolus--Levitin rate limit on local state transitions ($\nu_{\text{ML}}$)~\cite{margolus1998,mandelstam1945}, and reflectionless local unitarity ($U^\dagger U = I$)~\cite{landauer1961,bennett1973}---we formalize a bandlimited lattice EFT via four minimal axioms (Axioms A1--A4) under scale-generating nonlinearity ($[P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}}] \ne 0$)~\cite{lax1968}. Smooth, sub-Nyquist field configurations ($\Sigma(x,t) > 0$) propagate causally in $\mathcal{O}(1)$ parallel circuit depth per macro-step, whereas nonlinear convective steepening at a hydrodynamic shock front ($\Sigma(x,t) < 0$) saturates the local spatial bandwidth. Rather than postulating auxiliary degrees of freedom ad hoc, we prove via Stinespring and Sz.-Nagy dilation~\cite{stinespring1955,sznagy1953} (\texttt{Unitary\allowbreak{}Bandwidth\allowbreak{}Depth.lean}) that preserving local unitarity without violating spatial bandlimitation mathematically forces the existence of an orthogonal worldline clock fiber $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}} = V_{\Lambda}^{\perp}$ of rank at least $r = \mathrm{rank}([P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}}])/2$, a $90^\circ$ microlocal continuation in the complexified cotangent bundle across the sonic/Nyquist horizon $\Sigma(x,t) = 0$, and a localized Ginzburg--Landau shock-core defect-condensate potential $V_{\text{eff}}(|c|^2) = \Sigma_0 |c|^2 + \lambda |c|^4$~\cite{ginzburg1950} that is algebraically isomorphic to the Standard Model Higgs potential~\cite{higgs1964,tooby2024} under $\mu^2 = -\Sigma_0$. This monograph presents the unified axiomatic architecture, the five-phase analytical proofs, six machine-checked Lean 4 modules (\texttt{Mathlib}, \texttt{Physlib}, and \texttt{QuantumInfo})~\cite{mathlib2020,tooby2024}, four progressive hydrodynamic lattice benchmarks, the holographic Ryu--Takayanagi bulk-extrusion correspondence~\cite{ryutakayanagi2006,swingle2012}, and the Ginzburg--Landau--Higgs defect-condensate duality.
\end{abstract}

\bigskip
\section*{Executive Synthesis: Motivating Physical Cutoffs and Deductive Architecture}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Executive Synthesis: Motivating Physical Cutoffs and Deductive Architecture}
The Bekenstein--Nyquist Shock Worldline Conjecture investigates a bandlimited lattice effective field theory (EFT) motivated by simultaneous enforcement of three foundational principles of fundamental physics that have historically been analyzed in isolation:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The Bekenstein--Kempf Spatial Bandwidth Cutoff ($\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$):} Black hole thermodynamics and algebraic quantum field theory establish the Bekenstein bound~\cite{bekenstein1981,casini2008}, restricting the maximum information $I_{\max}$ storable within a region of radius $R$ and energy $E$ to
\begin{equation}
I_{\max} \le \frac{2\pi E R}{\hbar c \ln 2}.
\end{equation}
Following Achim Kempf's covariant bandlimitation framework~\cite{kempf2000,kempf2004,pye2015}, we treat a finite local spatial information density as motivating an effective ultraviolet wavenumber cutoff $|k| \le \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$, restricting observable continuum fields to a Paley--Wiener Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} = PW_{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}}(\mathbb{R})$ obeying Bernstein's inequality $\|u^{(r)}\|_2 \le \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}^r \|u\|_2$. By the Shannon--Nyquist sampling theorem~\cite{shannon1949}, $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ is unitarily isomorphic to a discrete sampling lattice $\ell^2(\mathbb{Z}_{\Delta x})$ of fundamental spacing $\Delta x = \pi / \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$.
\item \textbf{The Margolus--Levitin Rate Limit on State Changes ($\nu_{\text{ML}}$):} Quantum mechanics enforces the Mandelstam--Tamm~\cite{mandelstam1945} and Margolus--Levitin~\cite{margolus1998,lloyd2000} quantum speed limits, which bound the maximum frequency of distinguishable transitions between orthogonal quantum states by
\begin{equation}
\nu_{\text{ML}} \le \frac{2E}{\pi \hbar}, \qquad \Delta\tau_{\text{ML}} = \frac{\pi}{\nu_{\text{ML}}} \ge \frac{\pi^2 \hbar}{2E}.
\end{equation}
Within our bandlimited lattice EFT, the spatial cutoff $\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ and the local gate-rate ceiling $\nu_{\text{ML}}$ act as fixed ultraviolet/rate parameters defining a local spacetime information-processing capacity per unit length per unit time:
\begin{equation}
C_{\text{self}} \equiv \theta_{\text{Nyq}} = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \nu_{\text{ML}}.
\end{equation}
\item \textbf{Local Microscopic Unitarity vs.\ Landauer Erasure ($U^\dagger U = I$):} Fundamental quantum evolution is strictly unitary and causally local (bounded spatial fan-in radius $r_0$ per parallel step~\cite{liebrobinson1972}), conserving total Hilbert-space norm and phase-space volume~\cite{bennett1973}. While linear dispersive waves propagate trivially inside $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$, nonlinear convective steepening (such as $u\partial_x u$ in Burgers/Euler hydrodynamics~\cite{lax1973,whitham1974}) multiplies modes in the upper half of the Brillouin zone ($\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}/2 < |k| \le \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$), driving local ultraviolet spillover flux $\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t) = \|P_{\text{UV}}(u\partial_x u)(x,t)\|$ toward the forbidden super-Nyquist zone $(\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}, 2\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}]$. Standard continuum hydrodynamics stabilizes shocks via viscous dissipation~\cite{lax1973} (violating unitarity via Landauer bit erasure $E_{\min} = n k_B T \ln 2$~\cite{landauer1961}), whereas naive unitary lattice truncation reflects ultraviolet modes back into the bulk via Umklapp aliasing (producing dispersive Gibbs/Lax--Levermore oscillations~\cite{laxlevermore1983}).
\end{enumerate}
The central thesis of this monograph is that a bandlimited unitary system resolves nonlinear shock steepening under these three constraints without Landauer erasure or Gibbs aliasing by dynamically trading \textbf{spatial parallelism} for \textbf{sequential temporal circuit depth} along the lower-dimensional shock worldline.

\section{Axiomatic Foundations, Deductive Structure, and the Aliasing--Unitarity No-Go Lemma (Phase 1)}
\label{sec:phase1}
\subsection{The Minimal Four-Axiom Formulation (Emergent Dilation Architecture)}
\label{subsec:minimal-axioms}
To separate physical resource inputs from derived mathematical structures, we formulate the conjecture on a composite Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}}$ governed by four primitive axioms: two kinematic resource cutoffs (Bekenstein--Kempf and Margolus--Levitin), one dynamical nonlinearity condition, and one local unitarity conservation law.
\begin{axiom}[Spatial Bandlimitation --- The Bekenstein--Kempf Projector (A1)]
\label{ax:a1}
Observable spatial fields reside in a Paley--Wiener subspace $V_{\Lambda} = P_{\Lambda}\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}} = PW_{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}}(\mathbb{R})$~\cite{kempf2004,pye2015} defined by an idempotent, self-adjoint ultraviolet wavenumber projector $P_{\Lambda}^2 = P_{\Lambda} = P_{\Lambda}^\dagger$ with hard spectral cutoff $|k| \le \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$, unitarily isomorphic to the discrete sampling lattice $\ell^2(\mathbb{Z}_{\Delta x})$ with fundamental spacing $\Delta x = \pi / \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$~\cite{shannon1949}.
\end{axiom}
\begin{axiom}[Rate Limit on State Changes --- The Margolus--Levitin Sequential Channel (A2)]
\label{ax:a2}
Any local state transition coupling the bandlimited spatial subspace $V_{\Lambda}$ to an orthogonal degree of freedom proceeds through elementary rank-1 quantum channels ($\mathrm{rank}((I - P_{\Lambda}) W_k P_{\Lambda}) \le 1$) whose transition frequency is bounded above by the Margolus--Levitin rate parameter $\nu_{\text{ML}} \le \frac{2E}{\pi\hbar}$~\cite{margolus1998,mandelstam1945} (imposing a minimum sequential clock tick $\Delta\tau_{\text{ML}} = \pi / \nu_{\text{ML}}$ and maximum single-step rotation angle $\vartheta_{\max}$ per elementary gate $W_k$).
\end{axiom}
\begin{axiom}[Scale-Generating Nonlinearity --- Non-Commutativity (A3)]
\label{ax:a3}
The microscopic generator of spatial wave transport is nonlinear (convective mode-multiplying), so for steepening wave profiles the unconstrained short-time propagator $U_{\text{nl}} = \exp(\Delta t M(u))$ fails to commute with the spatial bandlimit projector $P_{\Lambda}$~\cite{lax1968}:
\begin{equation}
[P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}}] \ne 0 \quad \Longleftrightarrow \quad \mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}[u] \equiv \left\| \big[ P_{\Lambda}, [M(u), L(u)] \big] \right\|_{\text{HS}} > 0,
\end{equation}
where $(L(u), M(u))$ denotes the Lax pair~\cite{lax1968} of the nonlinear flow and $F_{tx}[u] = \partial_t M - \partial_x L + [M, L]$ is the zero-curvature defect, with pointwise ultraviolet spillover envelope density $\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t) = \|P_{\text{UV}}(u\partial_x u)(x,t)\|$.
\end{axiom}
\begin{axiom}[Reflectionless Local Unitarity --- No Erasure and No Aliasing (A4)]
\label{ax:a4}
Time evolution on $\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}}$ is generated by bounded-fan-in local unitary operators ($U^\dagger U = I$, forbidding dissipative Landauer bit erasure $E_{\min} = n k_B T \ln 2$~\cite{landauer1961,bennett1973}) that satisfy reflectionless impedance matching at the Brillouin boundary $|k| = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ (forbidding unphysical Umklapp spectral folding $k \mapsto k - 2\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ or backward dispersive Gibbs reflections into $V_{\Lambda}$).
\end{axiom}
\subsection{Deductive Emergence of the Clock Fiber, $90^\circ$ Rotor, and Defect-Condensate Potential}
\label{subsec:deductive-chain}
Under Axioms A1--A4, the auxiliary structures of the Bekenstein--Nyquist framework are not assumed ad hoc; they emerge as rigorous mathematical deductions along a single linear chain:
\begin{equation}
\small
\underbrace{\text{Axioms A1 + A3}}_{\text{Bandlimit + Nonlinearity}} \implies \underbrace{[P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}}] \ne 0}_{\text{Phase 1 Defect}} \xrightarrow{+\text{A4}} \underbrace{V_{\Lambda}^{\perp} \equiv \mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}}_{\text{Phase 5 Clock Fiber}} \xrightarrow{+\text{A2}} \underbrace{\mathcal{D}(\gamma) \text{ \& } \Sigma(x,t) < 0}_{\text{Phases 2--4 Rotor \& Condensate}}.
\end{equation}
Specifically:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Deduction 1 --- Existence of the Orthogonal Worldline Clock Fiber $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ (From A1 + A3 + A4):} By Axiom A3 ($[P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}}] \ne 0$), the projected spatial update $\Phi_{\Delta t} = P_{\Lambda} U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda}$ satisfies $(P_{\Lambda} U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda})^\dagger (P_{\Lambda} U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda}) = P_{\Lambda} - A^\dagger A$ with inter-band leakage $A = (I - P_{\Lambda}) U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda} \ne 0$, making $\Phi_{\Delta t}$ a strict contraction on $V_{\Lambda}$. By Axiom A4 (Unitarity) and the Sz.-Nagy/Stinespring dilation theorem~\cite{sznagy1953,stinespring1955} (machine-checked in Phase 5, \texttt{Unitary\allowbreak{}Bandwidth\allowbreak{}Depth.lean}), restoring unitarity mathematically requires an orthogonal complement $V_{\Lambda}^{\perp} = (I - P_{\Lambda})\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}} \equiv \mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ of dimension at least $r = \mathrm{rank}(A) = \frac{1}{2}\mathrm{rank}([P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}}])$.
\item \textbf{Deduction 2 --- Sequential Depth Lower Bound $\mathcal{D}(\gamma)$ (From Deduction 1 + A2):} Combining the commutator rank identity $\mathrm{rank}([P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}}]) = 2r$ with Axiom A2 forces any multi-wire unitary circuit realization $U_D = W_D \cdots W_1$ to have total gate count $D \ge r = \frac{1}{2}\mathrm{rank}([P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}} ])$ (\texttt{Unitary\allowbreak{}Bandwidth\allowbreak{}Depth.lean}), while on a single bond ($r = 1$) achieving a cumulative target Givens rotation angle $\Theta$ using elementary gates of step angle $\vartheta \le \vartheta_{\max}$ requires $D \ge \lceil \Theta / \vartheta_{\max} \rceil$ sequential sub-steps (\texttt{single\_\allowbreak{}bond\_\allowbreak{}angle\_\allowbreak{}substepping\_\allowbreak{}bound} in \texttt{Nyquist\allowbreak{}Rotor\allowbreak{}Gates.lean}), yielding the continuum Sub-Stepping Depth Theorem along the shock worldline $\gamma$ (Phase 3).
\item \textbf{Deduction 3 --- Normalized Feasibility Margin $\Sigma(x,t)$ and the $90^\circ$ Continuation (From A1--A4):} Identifying the local parallel channel capacity $C_{\text{self}} = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}\nu_{\text{ML}}$ (from A1 and A2) and the pointwise nonlinear spectral spillover density $R_{\text{self}}(x,t) = \mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t)$ (from A3) defines the dimensionless channel feasibility margin $\mathcal{M}(x,t)/C_{\text{self}}$, which is the Bekenstein--Nyquist Saturation Functional $\Sigma(x,t) = 1 - \frac{\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t)}{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}\nu_{\text{ML}}}$ (Phase 2). When $\Sigma > 0$ ($R_{\text{self}} < C_{\text{self}}$), spatial acoustic propagation is causally hyperbolic on $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ and executes in $\mathcal{O}(1)$ parallel stencil depth per macro-step; when $\Sigma < 0$ ($R_{\text{self}} > C_{\text{self}}$), purely spatial hyperbolicity breaks down at gradient catastrophe and Axiom A4 forces overflowing modes into sequential phase winding in $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ via the $90^\circ$ continuation $x \mapsto x + i c_{\text{eff}}\tau$, $\Delta k \mapsto -i\Delta k$.
\item \textbf{Deduction 4 --- Impedance-Matched Givens Rotor and Ginzburg--Landau Defect Condensate (From A3 + A4):} On the $2\times 2$ subspace spanning the overflowing Nyquist bond mode $|u_{b,\text{UV}}\rangle \in V_{\Lambda}$ and the active clock wire $|c_{j,k}\rangle \in V_{\Lambda}^{\perp}$, reflectionless unitarity at $|k| = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ (Axiom A4) fixes the $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Givens rotation angle $\tan\vartheta = \sqrt{\Delta k / \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}}$. Because transferring norm $|c|^2$ into $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ reduces the local spatial spillover overload linearly ($F(|c|) = F_0 - \kappa |c|^2$), the saturation functional relaxes as $\Sigma(|c|) = \Sigma_0 + 2\lambda |c|^2$, whose integral with respect to $|c|^2$ yields the localized Ginzburg--Landau shock-core defect-condensate potential $V_{\text{eff}}(|c|^2) = \Sigma_0 |c|^2 + \lambda |c|^4$~\cite{ginzburg1950}, algebraically isomorphic to the Standard Model Higgs potential~\cite{higgs1964,lee1977} under $\mu^2 = -\Sigma_0$ (Phase 4 \& Phase B).
\end{enumerate}
\subsection{Axiomatic Equivalence: Emergent Dilation vs.\ Primitive Bipartite Bundle}
\label{subsec:axiomatic-equivalence}
Just as quantum mechanics admits mathematically equivalent formulations (the Schr\"odinger, Heisenberg, and path-integral pictures), the Bekenstein--Nyquist Conjecture can be axiomatized either by deriving $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ via Stinespring dilation~\cite{stinespring1955} (Axioms A1--A4) or by taking the bipartite fiber bundle $\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}} = \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} \oplus \mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ as a primitive geometric structure equipped with minimal horizon gating:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Axiom B1 (Global Bundle Unitarity):} Composite evolution $U_{\text{tot}}(\Delta t) \in \mathrm{U}(\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} \oplus \mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}})$ conserves total norm $\|u\|_2^2 + \sum_{j,k} |c_{j,k}|^2 = \mathrm{const}$.
\item \textbf{Axiom B2 (Sub-Nyquist Dormancy / Horizon Gating):} The inter-bundle coupling $(I - P_{\Lambda}) U_{\text{tot}} P_{\Lambda}$ vanishes wherever $\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t) \le \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}\nu_{\text{ML}}$ ($\Sigma(x,t) \ge 0$).
\item \textbf{Axiom B3 (Impedance-Matched Overflow Serialization):} Wherever $\Sigma(x,t) < 0$, overflowing ultraviolet modes in $\mathcal{H}_{2\Lambda} \ominus \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ rotate into $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ via sequential rank-1 $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ Givens gates at $\tan\vartheta = \sqrt{\Delta k / \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}}$, costing one Margolus--Levitin tick $\Delta\tau_{\text{ML}} = \pi/\nu_{\text{ML}}$ per gate.
\end{itemize}
\begin{proposition}[Theorem of Axiomatic Equivalence]
\label{prop:axiomatic-equivalence}
The Emergent Stinespring Dilation Axioms (A1--A4) and the Primitive Bipartite Bundle Axioms (B1--B3) are operationally equivalent on all spatial observables in $V_{\Lambda}$ and induce identical sequential depth lower bounds $\mathcal{D}(\gamma)$, horizon metrics $\Sigma(x,t)$, and effective potentials $V_{\text{eff}}(|c|^2)$. Moreover, taking $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ as a persistent co-moving bundle (B1--B3) naturally accommodates multi-step phase memory ($K = 64$ worldline clock slots in Milestone 4) and shock-core defect condensates ($|c_{\text{eq}}|^2 > 0$).
\end{proposition}

\subsection{Analytical Proof Architecture for the Nyquist--Lax Trichotomy}
\label{subsec:nyquist-lax-trichotomy}
To prove that spatial parallelism breaks down at a shock front under Axioms A1, A3, and A4, notice that ``local unitarity'' purely within the spatial Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ combines two distinct operational requirements: norm conservation ($\|\Phi_{\Delta t}(u)\|_2 = \|u\|_2$) and bounded-fan-in spatial locality ($\mathcal{O}(1)$ parallel circuit depth within stencil radius $r_0$~\cite{liebrobinson1972}). Theorem \ref{thm:nyquist-lax-trichotomy} proves that at a super-critical shock front, these two halves of spatial local unitarity cannot coexist with the Bekenstein--Kempf bandlimit (Axiom A1), forcing the relaxation of $\mathcal{O}(1)$ parallel depth into sequential time-stepping governed by the Margolus--Levitin rate limit (Axiom A2).
\begin{theorem}[Nyquist--Lax Trichotomy / Aliasing--Unitarity No-Go Lemma]
\label{thm:nyquist-lax-trichotomy}
Any single-step spatial update operator $\Phi_{\Delta t} : \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} \to \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ acting across a shock locus where $\mathcal{A}_{\Lambda}[u] = \|[P_{\Lambda}, [M(u), L(u)]]\|_{\text{HS}} > \Theta_{\text{DSW}}$ must violate at least one of the following three operational postulates:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Postulate 1 --- Strict Spatial Bandwidth (Bekenstein--Kempf Bound, Axiom A1):} $\mathrm{supp}(\hat{u}) \subseteq [-\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}, \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}]$ without dynamically allocating sub-Planckian spatial lattice nodes.
\item \textbf{Postulate 2 --- Norm Unitarity / Information Conservation (First Half of Axiom A4):} $\|\Phi_{\Delta t}(u)\|_2 = \|u\|_2$, forbidding artificial viscosity, WENO truncation, or Landauer erasure~\cite{landauer1961} of ultraviolet modes.
\item \textbf{Postulate 3 --- Constant Parallel Depth / Bounded-Fan-In Spatial Locality (Second Half of Local Unitarity):} $\Phi_{\Delta t}$ is computable in circuit depth $\mathcal{O}(1)$ purely within $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ with a spatial stencil fan-in bounded by a fixed sub-Nyquist radius $r_0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof Architecture]
Assume Postulates 1, 2, and 3 hold simultaneously on $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Step 1 (Donoho--Stark Uncertainty Barrier):} By the Donoho--Stark uncertainty inequality~\cite{donohostark1989} ($|T|\cdot |\Omega| \ge 1 - (\epsilon_T + \epsilon_{\Omega})^2$), a shock discontinuity compressed to spatial width $\delta x \sim \pi / \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ concentrates $\mathcal{O}(1)$ spectral weight at the Brillouin boundary $|k| \in [\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}/2, \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}]$, generating a non-zero inter-band leakage operator $A = (I - P_{\Lambda}) U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda} : \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} \to \mathcal{H}_{2\Lambda} \ominus \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$.
\item \textbf{Step 2 (Unitary Defect Identity):} Decomposing the unconstrained unitary propagator $U_{\text{nl}} = \exp(\Delta t M(u))$ on $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} \oplus \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{\perp}$ yields $(P_{\Lambda} U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda})^\dagger (P_{\Lambda} U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda}) = P_{\Lambda} - A^\dagger A$. Thus, any bandlimited update $\Phi_{\Delta t} = P_{\Lambda} U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda}$ dissipates norm $\|\Phi_{\Delta t} u\|_2^2 = \|u\|_2^2 - \|A u\|_2^2 < \|u\|_2^2$ (violating Postulate 2) whenever $A u \ne 0$.
\item \textbf{Step 3 (GNVW Chiral Index Obstruction and Group Velocity Reversal):} By the Gross--Nesme--Vogts--Werner (GNVW) index theorem for 1D Quantum Cellular Automata~\cite{gnvw2012}, any strictly local constant-depth unitary circuit restricted purely to $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ (Postulate 3) has a trivial inter-subspace flow index between $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ and $\mathcal{H}_{2\Lambda} \ominus \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$. Folding the chiral ultraviolet flux back into $[-\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}, \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}]$ via Umklapp aliasing ($k \mapsto k - 2\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$) reverses the group velocity $v_g = \partial\omega/\partial k < 0$ at $|k| = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ and produces non-physical backward dispersive ringing~\cite{laxlevermore1983}. Preserving both Postulates 1 and 2 therefore requires Stinespring dilation~\cite{stinespring1955} into an orthogonal register $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ across sequential depth $D \ge \mathrm{rank}(A)$ (violating Postulate 3) ticked at the Margolus--Levitin rate limit $\nu_{\text{ML}}$~\cite{margolus1998} (Axiom A2).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}
\section{Microlocal Analysis of the $90^\circ$ Causal Rotation (Phase 2)}
\label{sec:phase2}
\subsection{The Bekenstein--Nyquist Saturation Functional and Mixed-Type PDEs}
\label{subsec:phase2-saturation}
Once Phase 1 establishes that no constant-depth spatial operator on $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ can simultaneously preserve unitarity and bandwidth across a Lax curvature spike, Phase 2 constructs the dynamical escape mechanism forced by Axioms A1--A4: a microlocal continuation across the sonic/Nyquist horizon that rotates overflowing spatial modes by $90^\circ$ into sequential temporal phase degrees of freedom governed by $\nu_{\text{ML}}$.
\begin{definition}[Bekenstein--Nyquist Saturation Functional]
Let $\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t) = |w_{\text{analytic}}(x,t)| = \|P_{\text{UV}}(u\partial_x u)(x,t)\|$ denote the local spatial spillover envelope density of the zero-curvature defect $F_{tx}[u]$ (whose global spatial integral defines the Hilbert--Schmidt defect $\|F_{tx}\|_{\text{HS}}$). The local normalized feasibility margin $\mathcal{M}(x,t)/C_{\text{self}}$ of a bandlimited field $u(x,t) \in \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ relative to the spacetime information-processing ceiling $\theta_{\text{Nyq}} = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}\nu_{\text{ML}}$ is defined by
\begin{equation}
\Sigma(x, t) \equiv \frac{C_{\text{self}} - R_{\text{self}}(x,t)}{C_{\text{self}}} = 1 - \frac{\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t)}{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \, \nu_{\text{ML}}}.
\end{equation}
\end{definition}
On the spatial cotangent bundle $T^*\mathcal{M}$ with dual coordinates $(\xi, \omega)$ and characteristic bulk wave speed $c_0$, the effective bandlimited wave operator $P_{\text{eff}}$ has the generalized Tricomi--Keldysh~\cite{tricomi1923,keldysh1951} principal symbol:
\begin{equation}
\sigma_{\text{eff}}(x, t; \xi, \omega) = \omega^2 - \Sigma(x,t) \, c_0^2 \xi^2,
\end{equation}
which governs three distinct microlocal regimes:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Sub-Nyquist Bulk ($\Sigma(x, t) > 0$ --- Hadamard Well-Posed Spatial Hyperbolicity, $\mathcal{O}(1)$ Parallel Depth):} Where $\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t) < \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \nu_{\text{ML}}$, $\sigma_{\text{eff}} = \omega^2 - \Sigma(x,t) c_0^2 \xi^2$ has the physically causal \textbf{hyperbolic} acoustic signature $(-,+)$ with real spatial characteristic speeds $\frac{dx}{dt} = \pm \sqrt{\Sigma(x,t)}\, c_0$. The Cauchy initial-value problem is Hadamard well-posed and updates in $\mathcal{O}(1)$ parallel spatial stencil depth per macro-step ($m_j = 0$).
\item \textbf{Sonic or Nyquist Horizon ($\Sigma(x, t) = 0$ --- Parabolic Degeneracy Locus):} Along the codimension-1 caustic locus where $\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t) = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \nu_{\text{ML}}$, the effective spatial acoustic speed vanishes ($\sigma_{\text{eff}} = \omega^2$), forming a parabolic degeneracy surface analogous to a transonic sonic horizon~\cite{tricomi1923,keldysh1951} or an effective horizon $g_{00} = 0$.
\item \textbf{Super-Nyquist Shock Core ($\Sigma(x, t) < 0$ --- Loss of Purely Spatial Hyperbolicity \& $90^\circ$ Clock Regularization):} Inside the shock core where $\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t) > \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \nu_{\text{ML}}$, the $\xi^2$ coefficient flips sign so that purely on the spatial manifold $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$, $\sigma_{\text{eff}} = \omega^2 + |\Sigma(x,t)| c_0^2 \xi^2$ becomes \textbf{elliptic} with imaginary spatial characteristic speeds $\frac{dx}{dt} = \pm i \sqrt{|\Sigma(x,t)|}\, c_0$, signaling gradient catastrophe and Hadamard ill-posedness of unregularized bandlimited spatial transport. In the complexified cotangent fiber $T^*_{(x,t)}\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}$, applying the $90^\circ$ Wick-type continuation $\Delta\xi \mapsto -i\Delta\xi$ paired with $x \mapsto x + i c_{\text{eff}}\tau$ restores causal hyperbolicity $\omega^2 - |\Sigma(x,t)| c_0^2 \xi_{\tau}^2 = 0$ along the orthogonal internal clock fiber $\tau$!
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Complex Coordinate Continuation and Internal Clock Winding}
\label{subsec:phase2-analytic}
Across the turning point $\Sigma(x,t) = 0$, applying complex coordinate continuation $x \mapsto x + i c_{\text{eff}} \tau$ paired with the $90^\circ$ cotangent rotation $\Delta k \mapsto -i \Delta k$ maps an overflowing spatial mode $k = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} + \Delta k$ ($\Delta k > 0$) into sequential temporal phase winding along the internal worldline clock fiber $\mathcal{C}_j$:
\begin{equation}
\exp(i k x) = \exp(i \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} x) \cdot \exp(i \Delta k \, x) \xrightarrow{\; x \mapsto x + i c_{\text{eff}} \tau, \;\; \Delta k \mapsto -i \Delta k \;} \exp(i \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} x) \cdot \exp(-i \omega_{\text{sub}} \tau),
\end{equation}
where the internal sub-stepping frequency is $\omega_{\text{sub}} = c_{\text{eff}} \Delta k \le \nu_{\text{ML}}$.
\section{Complexity Theory and Sequential Depth Lower Bounds (Phase 3)}
\label{sec:phase3}
\subsection{The Sub-Stepping Depth Theorem and Holographic Shock Complexity}
\label{subsec:phase3-substepping}
Phase 3 translates the sequential clock sub-stepping generated in the $\Sigma(x, t) < 0$ shock core into lower bounds on Boolean and unitary circuit depth, linking the PDE horizon transition directly to the separation between parallel complexity ($\mathrm{NC}$) and sequential polynomial time ($\mathrm{P}$).
\begin{theorem}[Sub-Stepping Depth Theorem]
\label{thm:substepping-depth}
For any bounded-precision unitary simulator evolving a field configuration along a shock worldline $\gamma$, the minimum sequential circuit depth $\mathcal{D}(\gamma)$ is bounded below by the line integral of the projection-curvature commutator anomaly:
\begin{equation}
\mathcal{D}(\gamma) \ge \frac{1}{\pi \nu_{\text{ML}}} \int_{\gamma} \left\| [P_{\Lambda}, [M(u), L(u)]] \right\|_{\text{HS}} \, ds.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
This theorem exhibits a direct structural parallel with the \textbf{Complexity = Action (CA)} and \textbf{Shock Wave Geometry} framework in holographic quantum gravity~\cite{stanford2014,brown2016,maldacena2016}. In holographic shock wave geometries~\cite{stanford2014}, injecting shock waves into a bulk spacetime stretches the Wheeler--DeWitt (WDW) causal patch at a late-time rate governed by Lloyd's bound~\cite{lloyd2000,brown2016}, $\frac{d\mathcal{C}}{dt} \le \frac{2M}{\pi \hbar}$, while saturating the Maldacena--Shenker--Stanford (MSS) chaos bound $\lambda_L \le \frac{2\pi k_B T}{\hbar}$~\cite{maldacena2016}. Here, each unit of integrated commutator spillover flux requires at least one orthogonal Margolus--Levitin clock cycle to resolve unitarily.
The proof strategy proceeds via \textbf{two-party communication complexity} across the moving shock cut $x_s(t)$ partitioning $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ into left ($\mathcal{H}_L$) and right ($\mathcal{H}_R$) Paley--Wiener subspaces: computing $K$ alternating non-commuting Rankine--Hugoniot unitary phase reflections $R_K R_{K-1} \cdots R_1$ across the cut requires $\Omega(K)$ sequential message-passing rounds, forbidding compression into polylogarithmic $\mathrm{NC}$ depth $\mathcal{O}(\log^k K)$.
\subsection{Planar Circuit Value Problem (PCVP) Reduction, the Monotone Trap, and the $\pi$-Rotor Negation Gate}
\label{subsec:phase3-pcvp}
In $1+1\text{D}$ spacetime, shock trajectories form a planar directed acyclic graph (DAG) of genus $g = 0$. While Goldschlager (1977)~\cite{goldschlager1977} proved that the general Planar Circuit Value Problem (PCVP) over $\{\wedge, \vee, \neg\}$ is $\mathrm{P}$-complete, Limaye, Mahajan, and Sarma (2009)~\cite{limaye2009} proved that the \textbf{Monotone Planar Circuit Value Problem (MPCVP)} over $\{\wedge, \vee\}$ alone collapses into $\mathrm{NC}^3$ ($\mathrm{LogDCFL} \subseteq \mathrm{NC}^2$ when stratified). Consequently, purely dissipative classical shocks whose amplitudes only add monotonically upon coalescence cannot escape $\mathrm{NC}^3$.
To break monotonicity in a planar $1+1\text{D}$ shock network, colliding shock worldlines must realize a phase-coherent logical negation ($\neg$) gate via anti-phase ($\Delta\phi = \pi$) destructive clock interference $G_\pi(\vartheta)G_0(\vartheta) = I_2$ (demonstrated in Milestone 4 and machine-checked in \texttt{Nyquist\allowbreak{}Rotor\allowbreak{}Gates.lean}). We note that while Milestone 4 explicitly realizes and verifies this unitary $\pi$-rotor negation ($\neg$) primitive, completing a full end-to-end $\mathrm{P}$-completeness reduction for $1+1\text{D}$ hydrodynamics remains a conjectured reduction requiring auxiliary fan-out (shock-splitting) and arrival-time synchronization gadgets.

\subsection{Apparent Lax Shock Entropy vs.\ Zero Global Landauer Entropy}
\label{subsec:lax-entropy-paradox}
A classical paradox in shock wave physics is the tension between Lax's macroscopic entropy inequality~\cite{lax1973} (which requires irreversible kinetic energy loss $\Delta\mathcal{E}_{\text{spatial}} < 0$ across a compressive shock front to select the unique physically admissible weak solution) and microscopic quantum unitarity~\cite{landauer1961,bennett1973} (which forbids irreversible bit erasure and guarantees zero global von Neumann entropy production).
Within the Bekenstein--Nyquist bipartite architecture $\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}}$, both principles hold simultaneously:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Zero Global Landauer Entropy Production:} Because the joint fluid-plus-clock evolution $U_{\text{tot}}$ is strictly unitary, any pure initial state $|\Psi\rangle$ remains globally pure for all time, with global von Neumann entropy $S_{\text{vN}}(|\Psi\rangle\langle\Psi|) = 0$ (\texttt{bipartite\_\allowbreak{}pure\_\allowbreak{}global\_\allowbreak{}entropy\_\allowbreak{}zero} in \texttt{QuantumInfo\allowbreak{}Clock\allowbreak{}Bridge.lean}).
\item \textbf{Apparent Lax Entropy as Clock-Fiber Entanglement:} A coarse-grained observer who measures only the bandlimited spatial fluid $\rho_{\Lambda} = \mathrm{Tr}_C(|\Psi\rangle\langle\Psi|)$ traces out the orthogonal worldline clock fiber $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$, observing a strict spatial norm contraction $\|u(T)\|_2^2 = \|u(0)\|_2^2 - \sum_{j,k} |c_{j,k}(T)|^2$ and an apparent macroscopic shock entropy $S_{\text{vN}}(\rho_{\Lambda}) = S_{\text{vN}}(\rho_C) \ge 0$ (\texttt{spatial\_\allowbreak{}shock\_\allowbreak{}entropy\_\allowbreak{}eq\_\allowbreak{}clock\_\allowbreak{}entropy}) with bipartite mutual information $I(\Lambda : C) = 2 S_{\text{vN}}(\rho_{\Lambda})$.
\item \textbf{Causal Bottleneck and Mori--Zwanzig Memory Kernel Equivalence:} In computational mechanics and statistical physics, physical laws simultaneously act as \emph{information compression} (represented by the bandlimit/coarse-graining projector $P_{\Lambda}$, corresponding to Crutchfield's causal state bottleneck or $\epsilon$-machine~\cite{crutchfield1989,gu2012}) and as \emph{predictive calculators} (represented by the compressed propagator $P_{\Lambda} U P_{\Lambda}$). Machine-checked in \texttt{Unitary\allowbreak{}Bandwidth\allowbreak{}Depth.lean} (\texttt{mori\_\allowbreak{}zwanzig\_\allowbreak{}markov\_\allowbreak{}defect\_\allowbreak{}eq\_\allowbreak{}reverse\_\allowbreak{}mul\_\allowbreak{}forward} and \texttt{mori\_\allowbreak{}zwanzig\_\allowbreak{}markov\_\allowbreak{}defect\_\allowbreak{}rank\_\allowbreak{}le}), the 2-step non-Markovian prediction defect satisfies the exact operator identity
\begin{equation}
P_{\Lambda} (W U) P_{\Lambda} - (P_{\Lambda} W P_{\Lambda})(P_{\Lambda} U P_{\Lambda}) = \big(P_{\Lambda} W (I - P_{\Lambda})\big)\big((I - P_{\Lambda}) U P_{\Lambda}\big) = B_W A_U,
\end{equation}
whose rank is bounded above by both the forward Stinespring leakage rank $\mathrm{rank}(A_U)$ and the reverse leakage rank $\mathrm{rank}(B_W)$. Thus, autonomous Markovian prediction commutes with coarse-graining if and only if $[P_{\Lambda}, U] = 0$; when $[P_{\Lambda}, U] \ne 0$, the Mori--Zwanzig memory kernel~\cite{zwanzig1961,mori1965} required to close the macro-dynamics is literally the product $B_W A_U$ of the reverse and forward Stinespring clock-fiber leakage operators.
\end{enumerate}
\section{Computational Verification on Bandlimited Hydrodynamic Lattices (Phase 4)}
\label{sec:phase4}
\subsection{The $1+1\text{D}$ Unitary Nyquist Rotor Lattice (Experiment H.3 / Milestone 2)}
\label{subsec:milestone2}
To validate Phases 1 and 2 on a nonlinear hydrodynamic PDE without artificial viscosity or spectral aliasing, Experiment H.3 (\texttt{toy\_\allowbreak{}model\_\allowbreak{}2\_\allowbreak{}nyquist\_\allowbreak{}rotor\_\allowbreak{}pde.py}) constructs the \textbf{Unitary Nyquist Rotor Lattice} in IEEE 754 \texttt{float64}/\texttt{complex128} precision on a periodic $1+1\text{D}$ ring $x \in [-\pi, \pi)$ of $N_x = 2\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ spatial nodes with fundamental Nyquist spacing $\Delta x = \pi / \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$. Each spatial site $j \in \{0, \dots, N_x - 1\}$ is augmented with an $M$-level local worldline clock register $\mathcal{C}_j = \mathrm{span}\{|c_{j,1}\rangle, \dots, |c_{j,M}\rangle\}$, yielding the composite Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}} = \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} \oplus \bigoplus_{j=0}^{N_x - 1} \mathcal{C}_j$.
The solver evolves the inviscid Burgers conservation law $\partial_t u + \partial_x(\frac{1}{2} u^2) = 0$~\cite{lax1973,whitham1974} via three state-driven components:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Translation-Invariant $\mathcal{H}_{2\Lambda}$ Analytic-Signal Spillover Sensor:} At each step, $u \in \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ is zero-padded onto a $2\times$-oversampled Paley--Wiener grid $N_{\text{fine}} = 4\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ to evaluate $w_{\text{fine}} = u_{\text{fine}} \partial_x u_{\text{fine}}$. Retaining the positive super-Nyquist band $\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \le k_{\text{fine}} < 2\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$ defines the complex analytic signal:
\begin{equation}
w_{\text{analytic}}(x_{\text{fine}}) = 2 \, \mathcal{F}^{-1}\!\left( \mathbf{1}_{\{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \le k_{\text{fine}} < 2\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}\}} \, \mathcal{F}(u_{\text{fine}} \partial_x u_{\text{fine}}) \right).
\end{equation}
For a bandlimited step of jump $\Delta u$, $|w_{\text{analytic}}|$ scales organically as $c_{\infty} \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} (\Delta u)^2$ with $c_{\infty} \approx 0.264$ ($17.630, 34.580, 68.369$ at $\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} = 64, 128, 256$), determining $m_j = \lceil F_j / \theta_{\text{Nyq}} \rceil$.
\item \textbf{Skew-Symmetric Cayley Transport with Constitutive Subgrid Phase-Lag Closure:} Decomposing $u = \bar{u} + \delta u$ via a resolution-consistent binomial diffusion filter ($N_{\text{pass}}(r) = N_0 r^2$ in the grid refinement ratio $r = (\pi / 64)/\Delta x = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} / 64$, so that the physical Gaussian smoothing width $\sigma_x = \sqrt{N_{\text{pass}}/2}\,\Delta x$ remains invariant across $N_{\text{pass}} = 4, 16, 64$ at $\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} = 64, 128, 256$) applies a constitutive subgrid phase-lag closure $(1 + \gamma \tilde{\tau}_j)^{-1}$ to the wave-frame fluctuation $\delta u_j$:
\begin{equation}
w_{\text{eff}, j} = \frac{2}{3} \left( \bar{u}_j + \frac{u_j - \bar{u}_j}{1 + \gamma \, \tilde{\tau}_j} \right), \qquad U_{\text{spatial}}(\Delta t) = \left(I + \frac{\Delta t}{2} A(w_{\text{eff}})\right)^{-1} \left(I - \frac{\Delta t}{2} A(w_{\text{eff}})\right),
\end{equation}
where $A(w)^T = -A(w)$, guaranteeing $U_{\text{spatial}}^T U_{\text{spatial}} = I$ to machine precision ($\sim 10^{-16}$).
\item \textbf{5-Bond Unitary Haar--Givens Rotor Sweep:} Each active shock node executes $m_j$ sequential $2 \times 2$ unitary Givens rotations $G_{j,k}(\vartheta_{j,k}, \phi_j) \in \mathrm{SU}(2)$ coupling the 2-site Haar Nyquist difference mode $u_{b,\text{UV}} = (u_{b_0} - u_{b_1})/\sqrt{2}$ to a single 1D clock wire $c_{j,\text{slot}} \in \mathcal{C}_j$ at the impedance-matched angle $\tan\vartheta = \sqrt{1/\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}}$.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Spacetime Causal DAG Critical-Path Scaling and Asymptotic Tightness (Experiment H.4 / Milestone 3)}

\begin{equation}
\text{total\_physical\_givens\_rotations} = \sum_{n=1}^{N_{\text{steps}}} \sum_{j=0}^{N_x - 1} m_j(t_n) = |V(T)| - (N_{\text{steps}} + 1) N_x.
\end{equation}
Along the critical shock worldline $j_{\text{crit}}(t_n)$, the simulation tracks both (a) the \textbf{Unrounded Discrete Gate-Sum Lower Bound} $\mathcal{B}_{\text{thm}} = \sum_{n=1}^{N_{\text{steps}}} F_{j_{\text{crit}}}(t_n) / \theta_{\text{Nyq}}$ (verifying that integer ceiling sub-stepping $m_j = \lceil F_j / \theta_{\text{Nyq}} \rceil$ converges to the unrounded flux demand as $1 + \mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}^{-1})$) and (b) the \textbf{Physical Time-Integral Flux} $\mathcal{I}_{\text{flux}}(T) = \int_0^T F_{j_{\text{crit}}}(t)\,dt = \sum_{n=1}^{N_{\text{steps}}} F_{j_{\text{crit}}}(t_n)\Delta t = (\Delta t \, \theta_{\text{Nyq}})\,\mathcal{B}_{\text{thm}}$.

\subsection{The Bandwidth--Saturation Equivalence Principle and Holographic Bulk Extrusion}
\label{subsec:equivalence-principle}
The analytical and numerical structure of Phases 2--5 establishes an operational \textbf{Bandwidth--Saturation Equivalence Principle}:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Informational Horizon Geometry and Proper-Time Dilation ($\Sigma(x,t)$ as an Effective Metric Component $g_{00}$):} $\Sigma(x, t) = 1 - \frac{\mathcal{F}_{tx}(x,t)}{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \nu_{\text{ML}}}$ enters $\sigma_{\text{eff}}(x, t; \xi, \omega) = \omega^2 - \Sigma(x, t) c_0^2 \xi^2$ in structural analogy to the Schwarzschild temporal metric component $g_{00}(r) = 1 - \frac{2GM}{c^2 r}$, inducing a proper-time queueing lag $\Delta\tau_{\text{lag}}(\gamma) = \mathcal{D}(\gamma)\Delta\tau_{\text{ML}}$ across $\Sigma < 0$.
\item \textbf{Inertial Mass as Sequential Queueing Drag:} In the Galilean-invariant effective transport velocity $w_{\text{eff}, j} = \frac{2}{3} \big( \bar{u}_j + \frac{\delta u_j}{1 + \gamma_{\text{eff}}(r) \tilde{\tau}_j} \big)$ with $\tau_j = m_j \Delta\tau_{\text{ML}} / \Delta t_{\text{ref}}$, the constitutive closure factor $M_{\text{inert}, j} \equiv 1 + \gamma_{\text{eff}}(r) \tilde{\tau}_j$ acts as a state-dependent inertial drag multiplier decelerating the over-steepened wave crest to the Rankine--Hugoniot shock speed $s = [f(u)]/[u]$~\cite{lax1973}.
\item \textbf{Rest Mass as Coiled Ultraviolet Momentum in the Clock Fiber:} In the stationary sine-wave shock benchmark ($u_0(x) = -\sin(x)$ at $T = 1.35 > t^* = 1.00$), the shock worldline sits macroscopically at rest at $x = 0$ ($s_{\text{RH}} = 0.0000$, measured speed $0.0004$) while storing $\mathcal{M}_{\text{clock}} = \sum_{j,k} |c_{j,k}|^2 = 5.732697$ of ultraviolet $L^2$ norm in the stationary clock register $\mathcal{C}_{j_{\text{shock}}}$ ticking at $\omega_{\text{sub}} = c_{\text{eff}}\Delta k \le \nu_{\text{ML}}$.
\end{enumerate}
\subsubsection{Holographic Bulk Extrusion, Ryu--Takayanagi Min-Cut Wire Counting, and Effective $g_{00}$ Horizon Geometry}
\label{subsubsec:holographic-bulk-extrusion}
Combining the Bekenstein--Nyquist Saturation Functional $\Sigma(x,t)$ of Phase 2 with the machine-checked Stinespring dilation bound of Phase 5 (\texttt{Unitary\allowbreak{}Bandwidth\allowbreak{}Depth.lean}) reveals a dynamical mechanism in which both an emergent orthogonal bulk/fiber dimension ($\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$) and an effective horizon metric ($g_{00} \leftrightarrow \Sigma(x,t)$) are extruded by ultraviolet spectral overflow on the spatial boundary $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$~\cite{ryutakayanagi2006,swingle2012}.
\begin{corollary}[Boundary Overflow Drives Bulk Depth and Horizon Geometry]
\label{cor:rt-bulk-extrusion}
Let $u(x,t) \in \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} = PW_{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}}(\mathbb{R})$ evolve on a $1+1\text{D}$ bandlimited spatial lattice with Nyquist cutoff $\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}$, Margolus--Levitin transition ceiling $\nu_{\text{ML}}$, and composite Hilbert space $\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}} = \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda} \oplus \bigoplus_{j=0}^{N_x-1} \mathcal{C}_j$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Dynamical Extrusion of the Orthogonal Fiber:} In the sub-Nyquist causally hyperbolic bulk ($\Sigma(x,t) > 0$, $F_j \le \theta_{\text{Nyq}} = \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}\nu_{\text{ML}}$), the spectral spillover into $\mathcal{H}_{2\Lambda} \ominus \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ vanishes exponentially, leaving the internal clock fiber unexcited ($m_j = 0$) and the circuit depth flat ($\mathcal{O}(1)$). When nonlinear convective steepening breaches the Nyquist horizon ($\Sigma(x,t) < 0$, $F_j > \theta_{\text{Nyq}}$), the complex coordinate continuation $x \mapsto x + i c_{\text{eff}}\tau$ paired with the $90^\circ$ cotangent rotation $\Delta k \mapsto -i\Delta k$ rotates overflowing boundary modes $\Delta k = k - \Lambda_{\text{Bek}} > 0$ into sequential temporal phase winding $\exp(-i\omega_{\text{sub}}\tau)$ along $\mathcal{C}_j$, dynamically extruding an internal fiber of sequential depth $m_j = \lceil F_j / \theta_{\text{Nyq}} \rceil \propto \Lambda_{\text{Bek}}(\Delta u)^2$ localized along the $1\text{D}$ shock worldline $\gamma$.
\item \textbf{Operator-Algebraic Ryu--Takayanagi / HRT Min-Cut Bound:} Each local Haar--Givens update decomposes a lattice bond $(u_j, u_{j+1})$ into its coarse-grained mean $\bar{u}_j = (u_j + u_{j+1})/\sqrt{2}$ and Nyquist difference mode $u_{j,\text{UV}} = (u_j - u_{j+1})/\sqrt{2}$, acting as a local multiscale wavelet disentangler (analogous to a MERA tensor network~\cite{swingle2012}) rotated into sequential Margolus--Levitin proper time. By Theorem \texttt{unitary\_\allowbreak{}bandwidth\_\allowbreak{}depth\_\allowbreak{}tradeoff\_\allowbreak{}clock\_\allowbreak{}wires}, any unitary factorization $U_D = W_D \cdots W_1$ whose elementary gates $W_k$ couple $\mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}$ to at most a single $1\text{D}$ clock wire $C_k \subset \mathcal{H}_{\Lambda}^{\perp}$ ($\dim C_k = 1$) must sever a minimum number of clock wires along the worldline cut~\cite{ryutakayanagi2006} bounded by half the commutator rank:
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{rank}\!\big([P_{\Lambda}, U_{\text{nl}}]\big) = 2r &\implies D \ge r = \mathrm{rank}\!\big((I - P_{\Lambda}) U_{\text{nl}} P_{\Lambda}\big), \\[4pt]
\mathcal{D}(\gamma) &\ge \frac{1}{\pi \nu_{\text{ML}}} \int_{\gamma} \left\| [P_{\Lambda}, [M(u), L(u)]] \right\|_{\text{HS}} \, ds.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
\item \textbf{Apparent Shock Entropy and Effective $g_{00}$ Metric Warping:} Because total evolution on $\mathcal{H}_{\text{tot}}$ is strictly unitary ($\|u\|_2^2 + \sum_{j,k} |c_{j,k}|^2 = \mathrm{const}$), macroscopic hydrodynamic entropy production is the entanglement/norm leakage $\mathcal{M}_{\text{clock}} = \sum_{j,k} |c_{j,k}|^2$ obtained by tracing out the worldline clock fiber $\mathcal{H}_{\text{clock}}$ across $\gamma$. Under impedance matching $\sin^2\vartheta = (\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} + 1)^{-1}$, the per-step leakage along $\gamma$ converges to the scale-invariant fixed point $1 - (\cos^2\vartheta)^{m_j} \to 1 - e^{-c(\Delta u)}$, while the sequential queueing delay $\Delta\tau_j = m_j \Delta\tau_{\text{ML}}$ warps the effective principal symbol $\sigma_{\text{eff}}(x,t;\xi,\omega) = \omega^2 - \Sigma(x,t) c_0^2 \xi^2$ in structural correspondence with the temporal metric component $g_{00} \leftrightarrow \Sigma(x,t) = 1 - \frac{\mathcal{F}_{tx}}{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}\nu_{\text{ML}}}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}
\subsection{Two-Shock Collision Interferometry, Coherent Clock-Mass Annihilation, and the Ginzburg--Landau Defect Condensate (Milestone 4 \& Phase 3.2)}
\label{subsec:milestone4}
\subsubsection{Evasion of the Monotone Planar Circuit Trap via Orthogonal Two-Mode Haar Clock Readback}
To demonstrate that intersecting $1+1\text{D}$ shock worldlines physically realize the non-monotone logical negation ($\neg$) gate primitive required to break dissipative monotonicity~\cite{limaye2009}, Milestone 4 (\texttt{toy\_\allowbreak{}model\_\allowbreak{}4\_\allowbreak{}shock\_\allowbreak{}collision\_\allowbreak{}gate.py}) replaces the write-only pointer advance with a \textbf{co-moving $K$-slot resonant worldline clock register} ($K = 64$, indexed directly by $\text{slot} = (\text{clock\_ptr} + k_{\text{sub}}) \bmod 64$).
On each active bond $(b_0, b_1)$, \texttt{execute\_\allowbreak{}bidirectional\_\allowbreak{}givens\_\allowbreak{}collision} transforms $(u_{b_0}, u_{b_1})$ into the orthonormal 2-site Haar wavelet basis:
\begin{equation}
\bar{u}_{\text{in}} = \frac{u_{b_0} + u_{b_1}}{\sqrt{2}} \quad (\text{smooth local mean}), \qquad u_{\text{UV, in}} = \frac{u_{b_0} - u_{b_1}}{\sqrt{2}} \quad (\text{Nyquist difference}),
\end{equation}
and executes a three-stage orthogonal update on the 3-mode subspace $(\bar{u}_b, u_{b,\text{UV}}, c_{\text{slot}})$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Stage 1 --- Unitary UV Stabilization ($u_{b,\text{UV}} \leftrightarrow c_{\text{slot}}$):} Rotates the overflowing Nyquist difference mode $u_{\text{UV, in}} > 0$ into the worldline clock slot $c_{\text{slot}}$ by $\vartheta_1 = \min\!\big(\vartheta, \, 0.85 \arctan(u_{\text{UV, in}} / \max(0, c_{\text{in}}))\big)$:
\begin{equation}
\begin{pmatrix} u_{\text{UV, out}} \\ c_{\text{mid}} \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} \cos\vartheta_1 & -\sin\vartheta_1 \\ \sin\vartheta_1 & \cos\vartheta_1 \end{pmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} u_{\text{UV, in}} \\ c_{\text{in}} \end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
\item \textbf{Stage 2 --- Phase-Coherent Collision Readback into the Smooth Mean Mode ($\bar{u}_b \leftrightarrow c_{\text{slot}}$):} When the state-driven shock separation crosses $\Delta x_{\text{sep}} = x_2 - x_1 \le 2.4 \Delta x$, the relative rotor phase $\Delta\phi = \phi_2 - \phi_1$ governs $\gamma_{\text{phase}}(\Delta\phi) = \mathrm{sgn}(\sin(\Delta\phi/2)) \sin^2(\Delta\phi/2)$ and $\theta_{\text{rec}} = \min\!\big(\theta_{\text{cap}}, \, 0.40 \gamma_{\text{phase}} \arctan(c_{\text{mid}} / \bar{u}_{\text{in}})\big)$ (gated by a constitutive monotonicity-preserving TVD/MP plateau limiter derived from the smooth initial envelope bounds), rotating stored clock condensate $c_{\text{mid}}$ back into the smooth local mean mode $\bar{u}_{\text{out}}$ without exciting $u_{\text{UV, out}}$:
\begin{equation}
\begin{pmatrix} \bar{u}_{\text{out}} \\ c_{\text{out}} \end{pmatrix} = \begin{pmatrix} \cos\theta_{\text{rec}} & \sin\theta_{\text{rec}} \\ -\sin\theta_{\text{rec}} & \cos\theta_{\text{rec}} \end{pmatrix} \begin{pmatrix} \bar{u}_{\text{in}} \\ c_{\text{mid}} \end{pmatrix}.
\end{equation}
\item \textbf{Stage 3 --- Inverse Orthogonal Haar Transformation:} Maps $(\bar{u}_{\text{out}}, u_{\text{UV, out}})$ back to $(u_{b_0}, u_{b_1})$:
\begin{equation}
\begin{aligned}
u_{b_0}^{\text{out}} &= u_{b_0}^{\text{in}} + \frac{\bar{u}_{\text{out}} - \bar{u}_{\text{in}}}{\sqrt{2}} + \frac{u_{\text{UV, out}} - u_{\text{UV, in}}}{\sqrt{2}}, \\[4pt]
u_{b_1}^{\text{out}} &= u_{b_1}^{\text{in}} + \frac{\bar{u}_{\text{out}} - \bar{u}_{\text{in}}}{\sqrt{2}} - \frac{u_{\text{UV, out}} - u_{\text{UV, in}}}{\sqrt{2}}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Localized Ginzburg--Landau Shock-Core Defect Condensate and Isomorphism with the Higgs Potential}
Because Stage 1 transfers ultraviolet energy $|c|^2$ from $u_{b,\text{UV}}$ into the clock fiber, the active commutator defect decreases linearly as $F(|c|) = F_0 - \kappa |c|^2$, shifting the instantaneous saturation functional to
\begin{equation}
\Sigma(|c|) = 1 - \frac{F_0 - \kappa |c|^2}{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \, \nu_{\text{ML}}} = \Sigma_0 + 2\lambda |c|^2, \qquad \lambda \equiv \frac{\kappa}{2\Lambda_{\text{Bek}}\nu_{\text{ML}}} > 0.
\end{equation}
Integrating $\frac{\partial V_{\text{eff}}}{\partial (|c|^2)} = \Sigma(|c|)$ yields the effective Ginzburg--Landau~\cite{ginzburg1950} quartic free-energy functional governing the localized shock-core defect condensate:
\begin{equation}
V_{\text{eff}}(|c|^2) = \Sigma_0 |c|^2 + \lambda |c|^4 = \underbrace{\left( 1 - \frac{F_0(x,t)}{\Lambda_{\text{Bek}} \, \nu_{\text{ML}}} \right)}_{-\mu^2(x,t) \, \equiv \, \Sigma_0(x,t)} |c|^2 + \lambda |c|^4,
\end{equation}
where in the smooth sub-Nyquist bulk ($\Sigma_0 > 0$) the clock fiber remains uncondensed ($|c_{\text{eq}}|^2 = 0$), while inside over-steepened shock cores ($\Sigma_0 < 0$) a localized defect condensate $|c_{\text{eq}}|^2 = -\Sigma_0 / (2\lambda) > 0$ forms spontaneously to restore the sonic/Nyquist horizon $\Sigma(|c_{\text{eq}}|^2) = 0$. Under the sign identification $\mu^2 = -\Sigma_0$, $V_{\text{eff}}(|c|^2)$ is algebraically isomorphic to the Standard Model Higgs potential~\cite{higgs1964,lee1977,tooby2024}, with restoring curvature $\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial^2 V_{\text{eff}}}{\partial |c|^2}\big|_{|c|=|c_{\text{eq}}|} = -2\Sigma_0$ measuring the stiffness of the Nyquist horizon $\Sigma = 0$.
